**Step‑by‑step reasoning**

1. **Interpret the vernacular**  
   The phrase “Temple, Texas is near Belton, Texas” indicates that the two cities are close to each other but do not share a boundary or overlap. “Near” is a spatial proximity term, not a containment or intersection term.

2. **Consider geometry types**  
   Both entities are polygons (city boundaries). Therefore, the only applicable predicates are those that describe relationships between polygons: contains, within, touches, overlaps, disjoint, equals.

3. **Match the vernacular to a predicate**  
   - **contains / within** – would imply one city completely encloses the other, which is not suggested by “near.”  
   - **touches** – would mean the polygons share a boundary, which “near” does not imply.  
   - **overlaps** – would mean the polygons share a non‑zero area, again not indicated.  
   - **equals** – would mean the polygons are identical, clearly not the case.  
   - **disjoint** – means the polygons do not intersect (no shared area or boundary). This aligns with “near” because the cities are separate but close.

4. **Select the best predicate**  
   The only predicate that captures the idea of two separate polygons being close without touching or overlapping is **disjoint**.

**Answer:** disjoint

  [CoT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: disjoint
